Community

### Community Codebreaks

Explore practice prompts and historical research leads, then prepare a sourced contribution draft to share through the project issue tracker.

Practice Idea

#### Design a Lighthouse Cipher

Create a nautical puzzle by combining Polybius coordinates with a second transformation, and give another learner enough clues to reverse it.

Suggested difficulty: Intermediate

 [Start with the Polybius exhibit →](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/polybius.html>)

Study Topic

##### Breaking Confederate Vigenère

Study the historical brass-disk convention, then compare its repeated-key weaknesses with ordinary Vigenère analysis.

Techniques to try: Kasiski · Index of Coincidence

 [Open the Confederate Vigenère exhibit →](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/confederate-vigenere.html>)

Research Lead

##### Room 40 and the Zimmermann Telegram

Trace how accumulated codebook recovery and an older-code retransmission helped Room 40 read and safely disclose the telegram.

WWI · Diplomatic codebooks · Source links in exhibit

 [Open the Zimmermann exhibit →](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/zimmermann.html>)

Practice Idea

##### Create an Enigma Challenge

Choose rotor, ring, plugboard, and starting settings in the exhibit, encrypt a short dispatch, and challenge a classmate with a crib.

Suggested difficulty: Advanced

 [Open the Enigma exhibit →](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/enigma.html>)

Study Topic

##### Hill Cipher: When Linear Algebra Attacks

Use matrix inversion modulo 26 to see how enough suitably independent known-plaintext blocks can recover a Hill key and why linearity is the cipher's central weakness.

Technique: Modular matrix algebra

 [Open the Hill cipher exhibit →](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/hill.html>)

Historical Topic

##### The Uncracked Dorabella Cipher

Edward Elgar's 1897 cipher letter to Dora Penny remains unsolved after more than 125 years — an 87-character mystery in 24 unique symbols.

Status: Unsolved · Primary-source image linked in exhibit

 [Open the Dorabella exhibit →](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/dorabella.html>)

#### Draft a Fact or Write-up

Have a sourced historical fact, a challenge solution, or a cipher puzzle to share? This static-site tool downloads a text draft to your device. It does not transmit or submit anything. When ready, attach or paste the draft into a [GitHub issue](<https://github.com/systemslibrarian/cipher-museum/issues/new>) for review.

#### Embedded script 1 (JavaScript)

Source text, not a record of every rendered state. Any ebook URL is replaced by a stable omission ID; redacted code is for inspection, not execution.

```javascript

'use strict';
(function(){
  var form = document.getElementById('commForm');
  var success = document.getElementById('commSuccess');

  function downloadDraft(){
    /* Basic validation */
    if(!form.checkValidity()){ form.reportValidity(); return; }

    var entry = {
      name: document.getElementById('commName').value.trim(),
      type: document.getElementById('commType').value,
      title: document.getElementById('commTitle').value.trim(),
      body: document.getElementById('commBody').value.trim(),
      sources: document.getElementById('commSources').value.trim(),
      date: new Date().toISOString()
    };

    var lines = [
      'Cipher Museum contribution draft',
      '',
      'Title: ' + entry.title,
      'Type: ' + entry.type,
      'Author: ' + entry.name,
      'Created: ' + entry.date,
      '',
      entry.body,
      '',
      'Sources:',
      entry.sources || '(none supplied)'
    ];
    var blob = new Blob([lines.join('\n')], {type: 'text/plain;charset=utf-8'});
    var url = URL.createObjectURL(blob);
    var download = document.createElement('a');
    var safeTitle = entry.title.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, '-').replace(/^-|-$/g, '') || 'contribution';
    download.href = url;
    download.download = 'cipher-museum-' + safeTitle + '-draft.txt';
    download.click();
    setTimeout(function(){ URL.revokeObjectURL(url); }, 0);

    form.style.display = 'none';
    success.style.display = 'block';
  }

  form.addEventListener('submit', function(e){ e.preventDefault(); downloadDraft(); });
  document.getElementById('downloadDraft').addEventListener('click', downloadDraft);
})();
```

```javascript
    { name:'SIGSALY',              href:'ciphers/sigsaly.html',              era:'World War II',  year:1943,  type:'Machine',         key:'Thermal noise + OTP',       security:"Historical Only", hall:'XI', trust:{"key":"historical","text":"Historical Only","description":"Obsolete or historical system; notes distinguish cryptanalysis from capture or other operational compromise."} },
    { name:'Microdot',             href:'ciphers/microdot.html',             era:'World War II', year:1941, dateLabel:'1941 FBI account; earlier origins',  type:'Steganography',   key:'Physical size reduction',   security:"Historical Only",    hall:'XI', trust:{"key":"historical","text":"Historical Only","description":"Obsolete or historical system; notes distinguish cryptanalysis from capture or other operational compromise."} }
  ];

  var tbody = document.getElementById('cipher-tbody');
  var searchInput = document.getElementById('cipher-search');
  var typeFilter = document.getElementById('type-filter');
  var eraFilter = document.getElementById('era-filter');
  var securityFilter = document.getElementById('security-filter');
  var hallFilter = document.getElementById('hall-filter');
  var solvedFilter = document.getElementById('solved-filter');
  var countEl = document.getElementById('cipher-count');

  var eraClasses = { 'Ancient':'ancient','Medieval':'medieval','Renaissance':'renaissance','Baroque':'renaissance','18th Century':'renaissance','Early 20th Century':'wwi','19th Century':'19c','World War I':'wwi','World War II':'wwii','Modern':'modern' };

  function solvedStatus(c) {
    if (c.type === 'Unsolved' || c.hall === 'XII' || /\/(?:beale|kryptos|zodiac|cicada-3301)\.html$/.test(c.href)) return 'Open puzzles';
    return 'Other exhibits';
  }

  // Build rows
  CIPHERS.forEach(function(c, i) {
    var tr = document.createElement('tr');
    tr.dataset.index = i;
    var yearDisplay = c.dateLabel || (c.year === null ? 'Date not established' : c.year < 0 ? Math.abs(c.year) + ' BCE' : c.year);
    tr.innerHTML =
      '<td><a href="' + c.href + '">' + c.name + '</a></td>' +
      '<td><span class="comp-era era-' + (eraClasses[c.era]||'') + '">' + c.era + '</span></td>' +
      '<td>' + yearDisplay + '</td>' +
      '<td>' + c.type + '</td>' +
      '<td>' + c.key + '</td>' +
      '<td>' + (window.TrustLabels.badge(c.trust) || '—') + '</td>' +
      '<td>' + c.hall + '</td>';
    tbody.appendChild(tr);
  });

  // Sort
  var sortCol = 'year';
  var sortDir = 1;
  var ths = document.querySelectorAll('.comp-table th[data-col]');
  ths.forEach(function(th) {
    th.addEventListener('click', function() {
      var col = th.dataset.col;
      if (col === sortCol) { sortDir *= -1; }
      else { sortCol = col; sortDir = 1; }
      ths.forEach(function(t) { t.classList.remove('sorted-asc','sorted-desc'); });
      th.classList.add(sortDir === 1 ? 'sorted-asc' : 'sorted-desc');
      sortTable();
    });
  });

  function sortTable() {
    var rows = Array.from(tbody.querySelectorAll('tr'));
    rows.sort(function(a, b) {
      var ai = +a.dataset.index, bi = +b.dataset.index;
      var ca = CIPHERS[ai], cb = CIPHERS[bi];
      var va, vb;
      // An undated specification is not year zero; keep it after dated entries.
      if (sortCol === 'year' || sortCol === 'era') {
        if (ca.year === null || cb.year === null) return ca.year === cb.year ? 0 : ca.year === null ? 1 : -1;
      }
      if (sortCol === 'year') { va = ca.year; vb = cb.year; }
      else if (sortCol === 'name') { va = ca.name.toLowerCase(); vb = cb.name.toLowerCase(); }
      else if (sortCol === 'era') { va = ca.year; vb = cb.year; }
      else if (sortCol === 'security') {
        va = ca.security; vb = cb.security;
      }
      else { va = ca[sortCol] ? ca[sortCol].toLowerCase() : ''; vb = cb[sortCol] ? cb[sortCol].toLowerCase() : ''; }
      if (va < vb) return -1 * sortDir;
      if (va > vb) return 1 * sortDir;
      return 0;
    });
    rows.forEach(function(r) { tbody.appendChild(r); });
  }

  // Filter
  function applyFilters() {
    var search = searchInput.value.toLowerCase();
    var type = typeFilter.value;
    var era = eraFilter.value;
    var security = securityFilter.value;
    var hall = hallFilter.value;
    var solved = solvedFilter.value;
    var visible = 0;
    var rows = tbody.querySelectorAll('tr');
    rows.forEach(function(row) {
      var c = CIPHERS[+row.dataset.index];
      var show = true;
      if (search && c.name.toLowerCase().indexOf(search) === -1 && c.type.toLowerCase().indexOf(search) === -1 && c.key.toLowerCase().indexOf(search) === -1) show = false;
      if (type && c.type !== type) show = false;
      if (era && c.era !== era) show = false;
      if (security && c.security !== security) show = false;
      if (hall && c.hall !== hall) show = false;
      if (solved && solvedStatus(c) !== solved) show = false;
      row.classList.toggle('hidden', !show);
      if (show) visible++;
    });
    countEl.textContent = 'Showing ' + visible + ' of ' + CIPHERS.length;
  }

  searchInput.addEventListener('input', applyFilters);
  typeFilter.addEventListener('change', applyFilters);
  eraFilter.addEventListener('change', applyFilters);
  securityFilter.addEventListener('change', applyFilters);
  hallFilter.addEventListener('change', applyFilters);
  solvedFilter.addEventListener('change', applyFilters);

  // Initial sort by year
  document.querySelector('th[data-col="year"]').classList.add('sorted-asc');
  sortTable();
  applyFilters();
})();
```

 Special Exhibit · The Science of Breaking Codes

### Cryptanalysis Techniques

"To know how to defend, you must first know how to attack."

Ten approaches to classical cryptanalysis, with seven hands-on demonstrations. Success depends on the cipher, evidence, language, key and message length; no technique here defeats a correctly used one-time pad.

 [Open Codebreaker's Workbench →](<https://ciphermuseum.com/lab/workbench.html>) [Open Cipher Detective →](<https://ciphermuseum.com/cipher-detective.html>)

The Toolkit

#### 10 Techniques That Break Classical Ciphers

Technique 01

Frequency Analysis

Al-Kindi · Baghdad · ~850 AD

Long English samples often have frequencies near E=12.7%, T=9.1%, A=8.2%, with variation by text. A fixed one-to-one substitution preserves the sample’s counts while relabeling letters. Compare counts and sequences to propose mappings; frequency alone does not guarantee key recovery. Homophonic and syllabic systems need additional analysis.

English letter frequency distribution — the foundation of frequency analysis attacks.

[Caesar](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/caesar.html>) [Monoalphabetic](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/monoalphabetic.html>) [Homophonic](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/homophonic.html>) [Great Cipher](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/great-cipher.html>)

Technique 02

Kasiski Examination

Friedrich Kasiski · 1863

In repeating-key Vigenère, the same plaintext at the same key phase produces the same ciphertext. Repeated ciphertext fragments can suggest key periods through factors of their spacing. Accidental repeats also occur, so candidate periods need further tests.

Technique 03

Index of Coincidence

William Friedman · published 1922

Measures the probability that two distinct sampled positions contain the same letter. Long English samples often lie near 0.066; independent uniform A–Z text has expected IC 1/26 ≈ 0.0385. These are reference values, not bounds: an observed IC can range from 0 to 1. Coincidence statistics can suggest repeating-key periods, but do not identify a cipher by themselves.

[Vigenère](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/vigenere.html>) [Running Key](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/running-key.html>) Polyalphabetic

Technique 04

Crib-Based Cryptanalysis

Polish Mathematicians · WWII Bletchley

Cribs are hypotheses about plaintext, suggested by message formats, context or repeated phrases. Against Enigma, analysts tested those hypotheses against machine constraints and additional traffic. Known plaintext is an attack model that modern encryption must withstand; it is not automatically a fatal weakness.

Technique 05

Known Plaintext Attack

Universal · Classical through Modern

Known plaintext can expose a classical cipher's key. For a 2×2 Hill cipher, two matched plaintext/ciphertext *blocks* suffice when the plaintext-block matrix P is invertible modulo 26: gcd(det(P), 26) = 1. Otherwise, more observations or other analysis are needed. Enigma cribs impose different, rotor-machine constraints.

[Inline SVG: original diagram/icon markup]

```html
<svg viewBox="0 0 520 210" style="max-width:560px;width:100%;height:auto;display:block;background:#1a1a2e;border:1px solid #2e2e3a;border-radius:6px;" role="img" aria-label="Hill key recovery: HILL to TCOZ gives an invertible plaintext-block matrix P; K equals C times P inverse modulo 26"><rect x="20" y="40" width="110" height="44" fill="#141422" stroke="#e8d5a3" stroke-width="1.3" rx="5"></rect><text x="75.0" y="33" style="font-family:ui-monospace,monospace;font-size:9.5px;fill:#8b7355;text-anchor:middle">known plaintext P</text><text x="75.0" y="66.0" style="font-family:ui-monospace,monospace;font-size:12px;fill:#e8d5a3;text-anchor:middle;font-weight:700">HILL</text><rect x="20" y="110" width="110" height="44" fill="#141422" stroke="#C85A5A" stroke-width="1.3" rx="5"></rect><text x="75.0" y="103" style="font-family:ui-monospace,monospace;font-size:9.5px;fill:#8b7355;text-anchor:middle">its ciphertext C</text><text x="75.0" y="136.0" style="font-family:ui-monospace,monospace;font-size:12px;fill:#C85A5A;text-anchor:middle;font-weight:700">TCOZ</text><path d="M140 62 h40 M140 132 h40 M180 62 v70" fill="none" stroke="#c8b273" stroke-width="1.3"></path><line x1="180" y1="97" x2="222" y2="97" stroke="#c8b273" stroke-width="1.3" marker-end="none"></line><polygon points="230,97 220,92 220,102" fill="#c8b273"></polygon><rect x="238" y="75" width="120" height="44" fill="#141422" stroke="#d4af37" stroke-width="1.3" rx="5"></rect><text x="298.0" y="68" style="font-family:ui-monospace,monospace;font-size:9.5px;fill:#8b7355;text-anchor:middle">solve C = K·P</text><text x="298.0" y="101.0" style="font-family:ui-monospace,monospace;font-size:12px;fill:#d4af37;text-anchor:middle;font-weight:700">K = C·P⁻¹</text><line x1="366" y1="97" x2="404" y2="97" stroke="#c8b273" stroke-width="1.3"></line><polygon points="412,97 402,92 402,102" fill="#c8b273"></polygon><rect x="420" y="75" width="84" height="44" fill="#141422" stroke="#c8b273" stroke-width="1.3" style="font-family:ui-monospace,monospace;font-size:12px;fill:#c8b273;text-anchor:middle;font-weight:700">K</text><text x="262" y="178" style="font-family:ui-monospace,monospace;font-size:10px;fill:#8b7355;text-anchor:middle">two matched blocks: gcd(det(P), 26) must equal 1</text><text x="262" y="194" style="font-family:ui-monospace,monospace;font-size:10px;fill:#8b7355;text-anchor:middle">here det(P) = −11, so K = C·P⁻¹ exists modulo 26</text></svg>
```

Hill key recovery — HILL → TCOZ yields K = [[3, 3], [2, 5]] because the plaintext-block matrix is invertible modulo 26.

[Hill Cipher](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/hill.html>) [Enigma](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/enigma.html>) [Lorenz](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/lorenz.html>)

Technique 06

Hill Climbing Search

Modern · Computer Era

Start with a random key. Decrypt. Score the result using English language statistics — common digrams like TH, HE, IN. Make random changes to the key. Keep improvements, discard downgrades. Use multiple starting points to explore candidates. Such searches can attack substitution, Playfair and transposition, but may stall at local optima or favor incorrect plaintext.

[Inline SVG: original diagram/icon markup]

```html
<svg viewBox="0 0 520 236" style="max-width:560px;width:100%;height:auto;display:block;background:#1a1a2e;border:1px solid #2e2e3a;border-radius:6px;" role="img" aria-label="Hill climbing: greedy steps reach a local peak and stall below the global best"><line x1="20" y1="200" x2="500" y2="200" stroke="#555" stroke-width="1"></line><path d="M20 190 C 80 150, 110 92, 150 92 C 190 92, 210 150, 260 165 C 300 176, 330 60, 375 46 C 410 36, 460 90, 500 130" fill="none" stroke="#e8d5a3" stroke-width="1.6"></path><circle cx="60" cy="168" r="4" fill="#d4af37" stroke="#141422" stroke-width="1"></circle><circle cx="90" cy="140" r="4" fill="#d4af37" stroke="#141422" stroke-width="1"></circle><circle cx="118" cy="110" r="4" fill="#d4af37" stroke="#141422" stroke-width="1"></circle><circle cx="140" cy="95" r="4" fill="#d4af37" stroke="#141422" stroke-width="1"></circle><circle cx="150" cy="92" r="6" fill="none" stroke="#C85A5A" stroke-width="1.6"></circle><text x="150" y="74" style="font-family:ui-monospace,monospace;font-size:10px;fill:#C85A5A;text-anchor:middle">stuck: every single swap scores worse</text><text x="375" y="30" style="font-family:ui-monospace,monospace;font-size:10px;fill:#8b7355;text-anchor:middle">global best — never reached</text><circle cx="375" cy="46" r="5" fill="none" stroke="#555" stroke-width="1.3" stroke-dasharray="3,2"></circle><text x="260" y="224" style="font-family:ui-monospace,monospace;font-size:10px;fill:#8b7355;text-anchor:middle">key space, one small change per step  →</text><text x="30" y="20" style="font-family:ui-monospace,monospace;font-size:10px;fill:#8b7355;text-anchor:start">fitness (English-ness of the decrypt)</text></svg>
```

Hill climbing search — iteratively refining key guesses by scoring decrypted output against English language statistics.

[Substitution](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/monoalphabetic.html>) [Playfair](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/playfair.html>) Transposition

Technique 07

Simulated Annealing / Genetic Algorithms

Modern · AI-Assisted

Genetic algorithms explore populations of candidate keys; simulated annealing sometimes accepts worse-scoring moves. These methods can help attack classical ciphers, but time and success depend on the problem and settings. A high language score does not prove a correct plaintext.

[Inline SVG: original diagram/icon markup]

```html
<svg viewBox="0 0 520 236" style="max-width:560px;width:100%;height:auto;display:block;background:#1a1a2e;border:1px solid #2e2e3a;border-radius:6px;" role="img" aria-label="Simulated annealing: an accepted downhill jump escapes the local peak and reaches the global best"><line x1="20" y1="200" x2="500" y2="200" stroke="#555" stroke-width="1"></line><path d="M20 190 C 80 150, 110 92, 150 92 C 190 92, 210 150, 260 165 C 300 176, 330 60, 375 46 C 410 36, 460 90, 500 130" fill="none" stroke="#e8d5a3" stroke-width="1.6"></path><circle cx="60" cy="168" r="4" fill="#c8b273" stroke="#141422" ```
```

Simulated annealing — one possible escape from a local optimum; a finite run need not reach the global best.

[Double Transposition](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/double-transposition.html>) [Playfair](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/playfair.html>) [Hill Cipher](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/hill.html>)

Technique 08

Stepping-Switch Cryptanalysis

Frank Rowlett & Genevieve Grotjan · SIS · 1939–1940

Japan's Purple machine routed plaintext through banks of telephone-style stepping switches rather than rotors. The US Signals Intelligence Service had no machine to study, so they hunted statistical regularities in intercept traffic — looking for cycles in how the switches advanced. Genevieve Grotjan’s September 1940 insight was a crucial step in the team’s reconstruction of the switch behavior. SIS built an analog machine, enabling decryption of Purple diplomatic messages. MAGIC was the wider intelligence product, not the name of this single attack.

[Purple](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/purple.html>) Stepping-switch machines

Technique 09

Computer-Assisted Manuscript Decipherment

Knight, Megyesi & Schaefer · USC ISI / Uppsala · 2011

Knight, Megyesi and Schaefer combined careful transcription, symbol statistics and linguistic reasoning to decipher Copiale. Their 2011 paper describes unsuccessful early automatic-decryption attempts, then progress after treating the Roman letters as spaces and grouping likely homophones. This was a combination of computation and human analysis, not an EM algorithm automatically discovering the answer. [Original Copiale paper](<https://aclanthology.org/W11-1202/>).

[Copiale](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/copiale.html>) [Homophonic](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/homophonic.html>)

Technique 10

Chaocipher Reconstruction

Moshe Rubin & George Lasry · 2010–2014

The disclosure of Byrne’s Chaocipher algorithm in 2010 made independent implementation and attack possible. Subsequent research studied known-plaintext and ciphertext-only attacks under specified conditions. Distinguish revealing an algorithm from recovering a secret starting state. [Researchers’ record of attacks and solved exhibits](<https://www.chaocipher.com/chaocipher-current-state-of-research.htm>).

[Chaocipher](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/chaocipher.html>)

⚡

**Search speed depends on the problem:** Short repeating keys and long, ordinary-language messages can be easy for automated solvers. Short texts, unusual vocabulary, layered ciphers and poorly chosen scoring models can remain difficult. The demonstrations here are examples, not measured performance guarantees.

📜

Al-Kindi’s surviving treatise describes frequency-based cryptanalysis. A monoalphabetic substitution preserves counts while relabeling symbols; polyalphabetic substitution changes that relationship, but need not make a short sample uniform. Language frequencies are evidence for hypotheses, not a guarantee of successful decryption.

Paste Vigenère Ciphertext (strip spaces)

Paste Ciphertext

—

Index of Coincidence

0.0385 (uniform reference) Reference comparison scale ~0.066 (English reference)

**Additive-cipher crib test.** Slide a guessed plaintext word across a Vigenère or running-key ciphertext. At each position this tool computes a possible key fragment, (ciphertext − crib) mod 26, with A = 0. A repeating fragment is a hypothesis to test against other text, not confirmation of the crib or key. [Enigma crib attacks](<https://ciphermuseum.com/ciphers/enigma.html>) instead test rotor and plugboard constraints; this subtraction is not the Enigma attack.

Ciphertext (letters only)

Crib (guessed plaintext word)  [Input cribWord: type=text; initial value=]

 Drag Crib

For an additive cipher such as Vigenère, known plaintext reveals the keystream *only at the matching positions*: Key[i] = (Cipher[i] − Plain[i]) mod 26. Align both inputs at their first letter. A repeating fragment suggests a possible period; finite observations do not prove that the rest of the key repeats.

Known plaintext prefix  [Input kpaPlain: type=text; initial value=]

Corresponding Ciphertext  [Input kpaCipher: type=text; initial value=]

Assumed repeating-key period (optional)  [Input kpaPeriod: type=number; initial value=]

To explore a hypothesis, enter a period no longer than the observed keystream. Any extension beyond the known plaintext remains unverified.

 Inspect Keystream

Reading: Joshua Holden, [*The Mathematics of Secrets: Cryptography from Caesar Ciphers to Digital Encryption*](<https://ciphermuseum.com/further-reading.html#holden>) (2017 edition), §§5.1–5.2, distinguishes repeating keys, running keys, and one-time pads.

Watch a small Caesar-key search score candidate shifts against English frequencies. It illustrates greedy search, not a guaranteed solve: a short or unusual message may score best under an incorrect key.

Ciphertext  [Input hillInput: type=text; initial value=KHOOR ZRUOG]

 Run Hill Climb

The objective is the mean reference frequency assigned to each letter: `sum(reference frequency of each letter) / letter count`. Higher scores are preferred. This measures a preference for common letters, not distance from an English frequency distribution.

Counterexample: `HELLOWORLD` and its reversal `DLROWOLLEH` both score 5.85; `EEEEEEEEEE` scores 12.7. A score optimum can be wrong even when every key has been checked.

 Restore supplied known-answer example

Simulated annealing sometimes accepts worse-scoring candidates to escape local optima. This Caesar example illustrates the rule; a finite run does not guarantee the globally best score or the authentic plaintext.

Ciphertext (Caesar)  [Input annealInput: type=text; initial value=WKLV LV D WHVW PHVVDJH]

 Run Annealing

The objective is the mean reference frequency assigned to each letter: `sum(reference frequency of each letter) / letter count`. Higher scores are preferred. This measures a preference for common letters, not distance from an English frequency distribution.

Counterexample: `HELLOWORLD` and its reversal `DLROWOLLEH` both score 5.85; `EEEEEEEEEE` scores 12.7. A score optimum can be wrong even when every key has been checked.

Historical Record

#### 12 Famous Codebreaks in History

Selected attacks and advances, with their evidence and limitations.

850 AD

Al-Kindi Breaks Substitution

Al-Kindi · Baghdad

Technique: Frequency Analysis

An early surviving systematic account of frequency analysis. Its effectiveness depends on the cipher and available text.

1850s

Babbage Breaks Vigenère

Charles Babbage

Technique: Repeating Sequence Analysis

Babbage worked on attacks against repeating-key polyalphabetic ciphers but did not publish his method. Kasiski independently published an approach in 1863.

1863

Kasiski Publishes the Method

Friedrich Kasiski

Technique: Pattern Repetition Analysis

Kasiski published a method using repeated ciphertext groups to investigate the period of repeating-key polyalphabetic ciphers. Publication did not make every polyalphabetic system immediately readable.

1932

Polish Mathematicians Break Enigma

Rejewski, Różycki, Zygalski · Warsaw

Technique: Permutation Analysis · Known Plaintext

Rejewski reconstructed military Enigma in 1932; Polish analysts subsequently developed several attacks and aids, including the bomba in 1938. Their July 1939 exchange with Britain and France shared methods and reconstructed machines.

1940

US SIS Breaks Japanese Purple

Friedman's SIS team · Grotjan's insight · Washington DC

Technique: Statistical Analysis · Machine Reconstruction

Purple provided valuable Japanese diplomatic intelligence. It did not reveal a complete operational plan for the Pearl Harbor attack; diplomatic and naval communications used different systems.

1974 · 1990

DES and Differential Cryptanalysis

IBM & NSA · rediscovered by Eli Biham & Adi Shamir

Technique: Differential Cryptanalysis

IBM’s designers knew a form of differential cryptanalysis while developing DES and used it in selecting the S-boxes. Biham and Shamir later developed the technique publicly. Its effectiveness depends on the cipher and the available data.

WWII

Bletchley Park Breaks Enigma

Alan Turing · Gordon Welchman

Technique: Crib Attacks · Electromechanical Bombe

Polish mathematicians reconstructed military Enigma before the war. Later Allied bombe machines used crib-derived constraints to eliminate settings. The intelligence contribution was substantial; a precise number of years removed from the war cannot be measured.

1941–1944

From the Lorenz Depth to Colossus

Bill Tutte · Tommy Flowers

Technique: Known Plaintext · Early Computer Search

Tiltman’s 1941 work and Tutte’s structural reconstruction preceded Colossus. The electronic machine became operational in February 1944 and accelerated statistical wheel-setting searches.

1943–80

VENONA: Reused Pad Material

US Army Signal Intelligence

Technique: Key Reuse Exploitation

Duplicated Soviet pad material enabled attacks on some traffic. Analysts also had to reconstruct codebook meanings; recovery was partial and often took years. VENONA’s success did not break the perfect-secrecy guarantee of a correctly used one-time pad.

1993

Linear Cryptanalysis vs DES

Mitsuru Matsui

Technique: Linear Cryptanalysis

Linear approximations enabled a statistical attack on DES under a large known-plaintext requirement. The often-cited 2⁴³ figure is a data requirement for a particular attack, not an unconditional reduction of all attack costs to 2⁴³.

1996

RSA Timing Attack

Paul Kocher

Technique: Side-Channel Timing Analysis

Kocher described how data-dependent execution times can leak secret information from implementations, including RSA. Such attacks require suitable observations and vulnerable implementations; they do not solve the underlying factoring problem.

2017

SHA-1 Collision (SHAttered)

Google · CWI Institute

Technique: Collision Attack

The SHAttered team produced two different PDFs with the same SHA-1 digest. This demonstrated a practical collision and reinforced a migration already underway; collision resistance and preimage resistance are different properties.

🔍

**The Big Pattern:** These cases combine mathematical analysis with **operational evidence**, **protocol weaknesses** and **implementation behavior**. Reused pad material, predictable plaintext and timing leakage create different attack opportunities. Predictable plaintext is not itself an error: modern encryption should protect it under the stated attack model.

#### From papers to inspectable evidence

[Evidence Workbench](<https://ciphermuseum.com/lab/evidence-workbench.html>) · [Copiale, Mary Stuart and Z340 investigations](<https://ciphermuseum.com/lab/decipherment-cases.html>) · [Modern attack conditions](<https://ciphermuseum.com/lab/modern-attack-evidence.html>)

#### Reading the search score

George Lasry, *A Methodology for the Cryptanalysis of Classical Ciphers with Search Metaheuristics* (2018 published thesis), §§3.2.1–3.2.4, printed pp. 29–34 / PDF pp. 50–55, and §4.5, printed pp. 56–57 / PDF pp. 77–78, distinguishes objectives, selectivity and resilience to key errors. The small Caesar landscape above is an original teaching example; it does not reproduce the thesis’s large-key experiments.

#### Reading

Joshua Holden, *The Mathematics of Secrets: Cryptography from Caesar Ciphers to Digital Encryption* (2017 edition), chapter 2 and §§5.1–5.2; David Kahn, *The Codebreakers: The Comprehensive History of Secret Communication from Ancient Times to the Internet* (revised edition, 1996); Robert L. Benson, *The VENONA Story* (Center for Cryptologic History edition, 2001), pp. 1–3, 7 and 14–17. The demonstrations illustrate hypotheses and search rules, not guaranteed recoveries.

#### Embedded script 1 (JavaScript)

Source text, not a record of every rendered state. Any ebook URL is replaced by a stable omission ID; redacted code is for inspection, not execution.

```javascript

function escapeLabText(text) {
  return text.replace(/[&<>"']/g, ch => ({"&":"&amp;", "<":"&lt;", ">":"&gt;", '"':"&quot;", "'":"&#39;"}[ch]));
}

function showTab(id, button) {
  const targetPanel = document.getElementById('tab-' + id);
  const targetButton = button || document.getElementById('tab-btn-' + id);
  document.querySelectorAll('.lab-panel').forEach(panel => {
    const isActive = panel === targetPanel;
    panel.classList.toggle('active', isActive);
    panel.hidden = !isActive;
  });
  document.querySelectorAll('[id^="tab-btn-"]').forEach(tab => {
    const isActive = tab === targetButton;
    tab.classList.toggle('active', isActive);
    tab.setAttribute('aria-selected', isActive ? 'true' : 'false');
    tab.tabIndex = isActive ? 0 : -1;
  });
  if (targetButton) {
    targetButton.focus();
  }
}

document.querySelectorAll('.lab-tab').forEach((tab, index, tabs) => {
  tab.addEventListener('keydown', event => {
    const previous = (index - 1 + tabs.length) % tabs.length;
    const next = (index + 1) % tabs.length;
    if (event.key === 'ArrowLeft') {
      event.preventDefault();
      tabs[previous].click();
    } else if (event.key === 'ArrowRight') {
      event.preventDefault();
      tabs[next].click();
    } else if (event.key === 'Home') {
      event.preventDefault();
      tabs[0].click();
    } else if (event.key === 'End') {
      event.preventDefault();
      tabs[tabs.length - 1].click();
    }
  });
});

// ── Frequency Analysis ──────────────────────────────
const ALPHA = 'ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ';
const ENG_FREQ = [8.2,1.5,2.8,4.3,12.7,2.2,2.0,6.1,7.0,0.15,0.77,4.0,2.4,6.7,7.5,1.9,0.095,6.0,6.3,9.1,2.8,0.98,2.4,0.15,2.0,0.074];

function buildLabFreqSVG() {
  const svg = document.getElementById('labFreqSVG');
  if (!svg) return;
  const bw = 16, gap = 6, h = 140, base = 155, maxEng = 12.7;
  let html = '';
  for (let i = 0; i < 26; i++) {
    const x = i * (bw + gap);
    const eh = (ENG_FREQ[i] / maxEng) * h;
    // English reference bar (outline)
    html += '<rect x="' + x + '" y="' + (base - eh) + '" width="' + bw + '" height="' + eh + '" fill="none" stroke="rgba(201,168,76,0.3)" stroke-width="1" rx="1"/>';
    // Input frequency bar (filled, initially zero height)
    html += '<rect id="svgbar_' + ALPHA[i] + '" x="' + x + '" y="' + base + '" width="' + bw + '" height="0" fill="#C9A84C" rx="1" style="transition:y .3s,height .3s;"/>';
    // Letter label
    html += '<text x="' + (x + bw / 2) + '" y="' + (base + 12) + '" text-anchor="middle" fill="#706878" font-family="monospace" font-size="8">' + ALPHA[i] + '</text>';
  }
  svg.innerHTML = html;
}
buildLabFreqSVG();

function labFreqUpdate() {
  const txt = document.getElementById('labFreqInput').value.toUpperCase().replace(/[^A-Z]/g,'');
  const result = document.getElementById('labFreqResult');
  const hint = document.getElementById('labFreqHint');
  const icEl = document.getElementById('labFreqIC');
  const reading = document.getElementById('labFreqReading');
  if (!txt.length) { hint.textContent=''; result.textContent=''; icEl.textContent='—'; if(reading)reading.textContent=''; resetSVGBars(); return; }
  const counts={}, len=txt.length, maxEng=12.7, h=140, base=155;
  for (const ch of txt) counts[ch]=(counts[ch]||0)+1;
  let maxCh='E',maxPct=0;
  for (let i=0;i<26;i++){
    const ch=ALPHA[i], pct=((counts[ch]||0)/len)*100;
    const barH=(pct/maxEng)*h;
    const el=document.getElementById('svgbar_'+ch);
    if(el){el.setAttribute('y',base-barH);el.setAttribute('height',barH);el.setAttribute('fill',pct>8?'#C9A84C':pct>4?'#9A7A40':'#7a6530');}
    if(pct>maxPct){maxPct=pct;maxCh=ch;}
  }
  // Compute IC inline
  let icSum=0;
  for (const k in counts) icSum+=counts[k]*(counts[k]-1);
  const ic=len>1?icSum/(len*(len-1)):0;
  icEl.textContent=len>1?ic.toFixed(4):'— (needs two letters)';
  const guessShift=(maxCh.charCodeAt(0)-69+26)%26;
  hint.textContent=`Most frequent: ${maxCh} (${maxPct.toFixed(1)}%) → If this is E, shift = ${guessShift}`;
  result.innerHTML=`<strong>Total letters:</strong> ${len}<br><strong>Most common:</strong> ${maxCh} = ${maxPct.toFixed(1)}%<br><strong>Caesar hypothesis if the most frequent symbol represents E:</strong> ${guessShift}`;
  if (reading) {
    reading.innerHTML = `Your text's most common letter is <strong style="color:var(--gold-lt)">${maxCh}</strong> (${maxPct.toFixed(1)}%). In many long English samples, the most common letter is <strong style="color:var(--gold-lt)">E</strong> (12.7%). If this is a monoalphabetic substitution, <strong>${maxCh}</strong> is a candidate for <strong>E</strong> in the plaintext; short or unusual samples may differ.`;
  }
}
function resetSVGBars(){for(let i=0;i<26;i++){const el=document.getElementById('svgbar_'+ALPHA[i]);if(el){el.setAttribute('y',155);el.setAttribute('height',0);}}}

// ── Index of Coincidence ────────────────────────────
function calcIC() {
  const txt = document.getElementById('iocInput').value.toUpperCase().replace(/[^A-Z]/g,'');
  const display = document.getElementById('icValue');
  const bar = document.getElementById('icBar');
  const interp = document.getElementById('icInterpret');
  if (txt.length < 2) { display.textContent='—'; bar.style.width='0%'; interp.textContent=''; return; }
  const counts={};
  for (const ch of txt) counts[ch]=(counts[ch]||0)+1;
  const n=txt.length;
  let sum=0;
  for (const k in counts) sum+=counts[k]*(counts[k]-1);
  const ic=(sum/(n*(n-1)));
  display.textContent=ic.toFixed(4);
  const pct=Math.min(100,Math.max(0,((ic-0.0385)/(0.066-0.0385))*100));
  bar.style.width=pct+'%';
  let msg='';
  if(ic > 0.080) msg='The observed IC is above the usual long-English reference. Repetitive or short text can produce high values; this does not identify a cipher.';
  else if(ic >= 0.060) msg='This IC is near the long-English reference. Plaintext, transposition and fixed substitution can all preserve this structure; other constructions can also produce it.';
  else if(ic >= 0.045) msg='This IC lies between common English and uniform-letter reference values. A repeating-key cipher is one hypothesis, not a diagnosis.';
  else msg='This IC is near or below the uniform-letter reference. Short samples can even have IC 0; the value does not prove randomness or a long key.';
  msg += ' Observed IC ranges from 0 to 1. The bar clips values outside its reference comparison scale. Consider message length, language and other evidence.';
  interp.innerHTML=msg;
}

// ── Kasiski Examination ─────────────────────────────
function runKasiski() {
  const txt = document.getElementById('kasiskiInput').value.toUpperCase().replace(/[^A-Z]/g,'');
  const result = document.getElementById('kasiskiResult');
  const hits = document.getElementById('kasiskiHits');
  if (txt.length < 20) { result.textContent='Enter at least 20 characters.'; hits.innerHTML=''; return; }

  const repeats={};
  for (let len=3;len<=6;len++) {
    for (let i=0;i<=txt.length-len;i++) {
      const seq=txt.substr(i,len);
      if (!repeats[seq]) repeats[seq]=[];
      repeats[seq].push(i);
    }
  }

  const found=[];
  for (const seq in repeats) {
    if (repeats[seq].length>1) {
      const positions=repeats[seq];
      const spacings=[];
      for (let i=1;i<positions.length;i++) spacings.push(positions[i]-positions[i-1]);
      found.push({seq,positions,spacings});
    }
  }

  if (!found.length) { result.textContent='No repeated sequences found. Text may be too short, or cipher is not Vigenère with a short key.'; hits.innerHTML=''; return; }

  // GCD analysis
  const allSpacings=found.flatMap(f=>f.spacings);
  const gcds={};
  for (const s of allSpacings) {
    for (let k=2;k<=Math.min(s,15);k++) {
      if (s%k===0) gcds[k]=(gcds[k]||0)+1;
    }
  }
  const sorted=Object.entries(gcds).sort((a,b)=>b[1]-a[1]);
  const likelyKey=sorted[0]?sorted[0][0]:null;

  result.innerHTML=`Found <strong>${found.length}</strong> repeated sequences. Top spacing-factor candidate (2–15): <strong style="color:var(--gold)">${likelyKey || "none"}</strong>. This heuristic counts overlapping repeats; accidental repeats and tied factors require further tests.`;
  hits.innerHTML=found.slice(0,8).map(f=>
    `<div class="kasiski-hit">"${f.seq}" at positions ${f.positions.join(', ')} — spacings: ${f.spacings.join(', ')}</div>`
  ).join('');
}

// ── Crib Dragging ───────────────────────────────────
// Require at least two complete periods. A match describes only this fragment;
// it does not establish either the guessed plaintext or the complete key.
function fragmentPeriod(fragment) {
  for (let period = 1; period <= Math.floor(fragment.length / 2); period++) {
    let fits = true;
    for (let i = period; i < fragment.length; i++) {
      if (fragment[i] !== fragment[i % period]) { fits = false; break; }
    }
    if (fits) return period;
  }
  return null;
}

function runCribDrag() {
  const ct = document.getElementById('cribCipher').value.toUpperCase().replace(/[^A-Z]/g,'');
  const crib = document.getElementById('cribWord').value.toUpperCase().replace(/[^A-Z]/g,'');
  const out = document.getElementById('cribResult');
  if (ct.length < 2 || crib.length < 1) { out.textContent='Enter ciphertext and a crib word.'; return; }
  if (crib.length > ct.length) { out.textContent='Crib is longer than ciphertext.'; return; }
  let html = '<div style="display:flex;flex-direction:column;gap:.4rem;max-height:300px;overflow-y:auto;">';
  for (let pos = 0; pos <= ct.length - crib.length; pos++) {
    let key = '';
    for (let j = 0; j < crib.length; j++) {
      key += ALPHA[(ct.charCodeAt(pos + j) - crib.charCodeAt(j) + 26) % 26];
    }
    const period = fragmentPeriod(key);
    const style = period !== null ? 'border-color:var(--gold);background:var(--gold-glow);' : '';
    html += '<div style="font-family:var(--fm);font-size:.75rem;background:var(--s3);border:1px solid var(--s4);border-radius:var(--r);padding:.5rem .75rem;color:var(--tx2);' + style + '">';
    html += '<span style="color:var(--tx3);min-width:60px;display:inline-block;">Pos ' + pos + ':</span> ';
    html += 'Key fragment = <span style="color:var(--gold-lt);font-weight:700;">' + key + '</span>';
    if (period !== null) html += ' <span style="color:var(--gold);margin-left:.5rem;">Period ' + period + ' fits this fragment (hypothesis)</span>';
    html += '</div>';
  }
  html += '</div><p style="margin-top:.75rem;color:var(--tx2);">A highlighted period fits at least two repeats within the guessed span. Check the proposed key against plaintext outside the crib. No highlight does not rule out a longer period or a running key.</p>';
  out.innerHTML = html;
}

// ── Known Plaintext Attack ──────────────────────────
function runKPA() {
  const plain = document.getElementById('kpaPlain').value.toUpperCase().replace(/[^A-Z]/g,'');
  const cipher = document.getElementById('kpaCipher').value.toUpperCase().replace(/[^A-Z]/g,'');
  const out = document.getElementById('kpaResult');
  if (!plain.length || !cipher.length) { out.textContent='Enter both plaintext and ciphertext.'; return; }
  const len = Math.min(plain.length, cipher.length);
  let key = '';
  for (let i = 0; i < len; i++) {
    key += ALPHA[(cipher.charCodeAt(i) - plain.charCodeAt(i) + 26) % 26];
  }
  let html = '<strong>Observed keystream (' + len + ' positions):</strong> <span style="color:var(--gold-lt);font-family:var(--fm);letter-spacing:.1em;overflow-wrap:anywhere;">' + key + '</span>';
  html += '<br><strong>Supported plaintext span:</strong> <span style="font-family:var(--fm);overflow-wrap:anywhere;">' + plain.slice(0, len) + '</span>';
  if (plain.length > cipher.length) html += '<br>Extra plaintext has no matching ciphertext and was not used.';
  if (cipher.length > len) html += '<br><strong>Unknown remainder:</strong> ' + (cipher.length - len) + ' ciphertext positions; the observed fragment alone does not decrypt them.';
  const period = fragmentPeriod(key);
  if (period !== null) {
    html += '<br><strong>Period hypothesis:</strong> ' + period + ' (fragment ' + key.slice(0, period) + '). This fits the observed span; it does not prove the complete key repeats.';
  } else {
    html += '<br>No period with two complete repeats fits the observed span. Longer periods and nonrepeating keys remain possible.';
  }

  const periodInput = document.getElementById('kpaPeriod');
  const requested = periodInput.value.trim();
  // Browsers expose unfinished number input (for example "1e") as an empty
  // value with badInput set. Do not mistake that state for an omitted period.
  if (periodInput.validity.badInput) {
    html += '<br><strong>Cannot test that period:</strong> enter a whole number from 1 to ' + len + ', or clear the field.';
  } else if (requested) {
    const assumed = Number(requested);
    if (!Number.isInteger(assumed) || assumed < 1 || assumed > len) {
      html += '<br><strong>Cannot test that period:</strong> enter a whole number from 1 to ' + len + '.';
    } else {
      const candidate = key.slice(0, assumed);
      const fits = Array.from(key).every((letter, i) => letter === candidate[i % assumed]);
      if (!fits) {
        html += '<br><strong>Period ' + assumed + ' rejected:</strong> it contradicts the observed keystream.';
      } else {
        let extrapolated = '';
        for (let i = len; i < cipher.length; i++) {
          extrapolated += ALPHA[(cipher.charCodeAt(i) - candidate.charCodeAt(i % assumed) + 26) % 26];
        }
        html += '<br><br><strong>Assumed key:</strong> ' + candidate + ' (period ' + assumed + ').';
        if (extrapolated) html += '<br><strong>Unverified extension under this assumption:</strong> <span style="font-family:var(--fm);overflow-wrap:anywhere;">' + extrapolated + '</span>';
        else html += '<br>There is no ciphertext beyond the known span to extend.';
        html += '<br>Validate this hypothesis against independent plaintext or other evidence.';
      }
    }
  }
  out.innerHTML = html;
}

// The score stays unchanged: this lesson exposes its limitations.
function caesarToyScore(text) {
  const letters = text.toUpperCase().replace(/[^A-Z]/g, '');
  if (!letters.length) return null;
  return Array.from(letters).reduce((sum, c) => sum + ENG_FREQ[c.charCodeAt(0) - 65], 0) / letters.length;
}
function caesarToyDecrypt(text, shift) {
  return text.toUpperCase().replace(/[A-Z]/g, c => ALPHA[(c.charCodeAt(0) - 65 - shift + 26) % 26]);
}
const CAESAR_LESSON_EXAMPLES = {
  hill: { ciphertext: 'KHOOR ZRUOG', plaintext: 'HELLO WORLD', shift: 3 },
  anneal: { ciphertext: 'WKLV LV D WHVW PHVVDJH', plaintext: 'THIS IS A TEST MESSAGE', shift: 3 }
};
function renderCaesarLandscape(prefix, input, state) {
  const out = document.getElementById(prefix + 'Landscape');
  if (!/[A-Z]/i.test(input)) {
    out.textContent = 'Enter at least one A–Z letter to define a score. No search or language conclusion is available.';
    return;
  }
  const rows = Array.from({length: 26}, (_, shift) => ({shift, text: caesarToyDecrypt(input, shift), score: caesarToyScore(caesarToyDecrypt(input, shift))}));
  const maximum = Math.max(...rows.map(row => row.score));
  const optima = rows.filter(row => Math.abs(row.score - maximum) < 1e-10).map(row => row.shift);
  const example = CAESAR_LESSON_EXAMPLES[prefix];
  const known = input.toUpperCase().trim() === example.ciphertext ? example : null;
  let html = '<h3>All 26 shifts under this objective</h3><p><strong>Search stopping point:</strong> ' +
    (state ? 'shift ' + state.stop + ' (last accepted state).' : 'not run.') +
    ' <strong>Score optimum after checking all 26:</strong> shift' + (optima.length > 1 ? 's ' : ' ') + optima.join(', ') +
    '. <strong>Supplied known answer:</strong> ' + (known ? 'shift ' + known.shift + ', ' + known.plaintext + '.' : 'none for this edited input.') + '</p>';
  if (state) html += '<p><strong>Best sampled shift:</strong> ' + state.best + '. <strong>Evaluated path:</strong> ' + state.path.join(' → ') + '. Rejected proposals are included; they do not become the stopping point.</p>';
  html += '<p>The exhaustive score check is a reference for this 26-key lesson, separate from the search trajectory. It does not validate a plaintext.</p>' +
    '<div style="overflow-x:auto;" role="region" tabindex="0" aria-label="Caesar shift scores; scroll horizontally if needed"><table class="caesar-landscape-table" style="width:100%;text-align:left;"><caption>Higher mean letter-frequency scores are preferred. Markers distinguish the search state, model optimum and supplied answer.</caption><thead><tr><th scope="col">Shift</th><th scope="col">Score</th><th scope="col">Role</th><th scope="col">Candidate</th></tr></thead><tbody>';
  for (const row of rows) {
    const roles = [];
    if (state && row.shift === state.stop) roles.push('Stopping point');
    if (state && row.shift === state.best) roles.push('Best sampled');
    if (optima.includes(row.shift)) roles.push('Score optimum');
    if (known && row.shift === known.shift) roles.push('Supplied answer');
    html += '<tr data-shift="' + row.shift + '" data-score="' + row.score + '"><th scope="row">' + row.shift + '</th><td>' + row.score.toFixed(4) +
      '<br><meter min="0" max="12.7" value="' + row.score + '" aria-label="Shift ' + row.shift + ' score" style="width:6rem;">' + row.score.toFixed(4) + '</meter></td><td>' + roles.join('; ') +
      '</td><td style="font-family:var(--fm);word-break:break-word;">' + escapeLabText(row.text) + '</td></tr>';
  }
  out.innerHTML = html + '</tbody></table></div>';
}
for (const prefix of ['hill', 'anneal']) {
  const field = document.getElementById(prefix + 'Input');
  function refresh() { document.getElementById(prefix + 'Output').textContent = ''; renderCaesarLandscape(prefix, field.value); }
  field.addEventListener('input', refresh);
  document.getElementById(prefix + 'Example').addEventListener('click', () => { field.value = CAESAR_LESSON_EXAMPLES[prefix].ciphertext; refresh(); });
  refresh();
}

// ── Hill Climbing ───────────────────────────────────
async function runHillClimb() {
  const input = document.getElementById('hillInput').value.toUpperCase();
  const out = document.getElementById('hillOutput');
  const btn = document.getElementById('hillBtn');
  if (!/[A-Z]/.test(input)) { out.textContent = ''; renderCaesarLandscape('hill', input); return; }
  document.getElementById('hillInput').disabled = true;
  document.getElementById('hillExample').disabled = true;
  btn.disabled = true;
  out.innerHTML = '';

  const score = caesarToyScore;
  const decrypt = caesarToyDecrypt;

  let current = 0;
  let bestScore = score(decrypt(input, 0));
  let steps = [{shift: 0, score: bestScore, text: decrypt(input, 0), action: 'Start'}];

  for (let iter = 0; iter < 30; iter++) {
    const neighbors = [(current + 1) % 26, (current + 25) % 26];
    let improved = false;
    for (const n of neighbors) {
      const ns = score(decrypt(input, n));
      steps.push({shift: n, score: ns, text: decrypt(input, n), action: ns > bestScore ? 'Climb ↑' : 'Reject ↓'});
      if (ns > bestScore) {
        bestScore = ns;
        current = n;
        improved = true;
        break;
      }
    }
    if (!improved) break;
  }

  for (let i = 0; i < steps.length; i++) {
    const s = steps[i];
    const line = document.createElement('div');
    const color = s.action === 'Climb ↑' ? 'var(--green)' : s.action === 'Reject ↓' ? 'var(--red)' : 'var(--gold)';
    line.style.cssText = 'padding:.3rem .6rem;border-radius:3px;';
    line.innerHTML = '<span style="color:' + color + ';min-width:70px;display:inline-block;">' + s.action + '</span> Shift ' + s.shift.toString().padStart(2,'0') + ' (score ' + s.score.toFixed(1) + '): <span style="color:var(--tx);">' + escapeLabText(s.text) + '</span>';
    if (i === steps.length - 1 && s.action !== 'Reject ↓') {
      line.style.background = 'var(--gold-glow)';
      line.innerHTML += ' <span style="color:var(--green);font-size:.7rem;margin-left:.5rem;">← Converged</span>';
    }
    out.appendChild(line);
    await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 120));
  }
  renderCaesarLandscape('hill', input, {stop: current, best: current, path: steps.map(step => step.shift)});
  document.getElementById('hillInput').disabled = false;
  document.getElementById('hillExample').disabled = false;
  btn.disabled = false;
}

// ── Simulated Annealing ─────────────────────────────
async function runAnneal() {
  const input = document.getElementById('annealInput').value.toUpperCase();
  const out = document.getElementById('annealOutput');
  const btn = document.getElementById('annealBtn');
  if (!/[A-Z]/.test(input)) { out.textContent = ''; renderCaesarLandscape('anneal', input); return; }
  document.getElementById('annealInput').disabled = true;
  document.getElementById('annealExample').disabled = true;
  btn.disabled = true;
  out.innerHTML = '';

  const score = caesarToyScore;
  const decrypt = caesarToyDecrypt;

  let current = Math.floor(Math.random() * 26);
  const initialShift = current;
  let currentScore = score(decrypt(input, current));
  let bestShift = current, bestScore = currentScore;
  let temp = 10.0;
  const cooling = 0.85;
  let steps = [];

  for (let iter = 0; iter < 30 && temp > 0.1; iter++) {
    const neighbor = (current + (Math.random() < 0.5 ? 1 : 25)) % 26;
    const ns = score(decrypt(input, neighbor));
    const delta = ns - currentScore;
    const accept = delta > 0 || Math.random() < Math.exp(delta / temp);
    const action = delta > 0 ? 'Better ↑' : (accept ? 'Accept ↔' : 'Reject ↓');
    steps.push({shift: neighbor, score: ns, text: decrypt(input, neighbor), action, temp: temp.toFixed(2)});
    if (accept) {
      current = neighbor;
      currentScore = ns;
      if (ns > bestScore) { bestScore = ns; bestShift = neighbor; }
    }
    temp *= cooling;
  }

  for (let i = 0; i < steps.length; i++) {
    const s = steps[i];
    const line = document.createElement('div');
    const color = s.action === 'Better ↑' ? 'var(--green)' : s.action === 'Accept ↔' ? 'var(--orange)' : 'var(--red)';
    line.style.cssText = 'padding:.3rem .6rem;border-radius:3px;';
    line.innerHTML = '<span style="color:var(--tx3);min-width:55px;display:inline-block;">T=' + s.temp + '</span> <span style="color:' + color + ';min-width:65px;display:inline-block;">' + s.action + '</span> Shift ' + s.shift.toString().padStart(2,'0') + ': <span style="color:var(--tx);">' + escapeLabText(s.text) + '</span>';
    out.appendChild(line);
    await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 120));
  }

  const final = document.createElement('div');
  final.style.cssText = 'margin-top:.75rem;padding:.6rem;background:var(--gold-glow);border-radius:3px;';
  final.innerHTML = '<strong style="color:var(--gold);">Best sampled candidate:</strong> Shift ' + bestShift + ' → <span style="color:var(--tx);font-family:var(--fm);letter-spacing:.1em;">' + escapeLabText(decrypt(input, bestShift)) + '</span> <span style="color:var(--green);font-size:.7rem;">(score ' + bestScore.toFixed(1) + ')</span>';
  out.appendChild(final);
  renderCaesarLandscape('anneal', input, {stop: current, best: bestShift, path: [initialShift, ...steps.map(step => step.shift)]});
  document.getElementById('annealInput').disabled = false;
  document.getElementById('annealExample').disabled = false;
  btn.disabled = false;
}
```

#### Embedded script 1 (JavaScript)

Source text, not a record of every rendered state. Any ebook URL is replaced by a stable omission ID; redacted code is for inspection, not execution.

```javascript

  // Chrome-less embeddable demo: /embed.html?cipher=caesar
  // The cipher name is validated against a strict pattern, then handed to the
  // same demo-loader that renders exhibit-page demos.
  (function () {
    'use strict';
    var cipher = new URLSearchParams(location.search).get('cipher') || '';
    var valid = /^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9]{0,39}$/.test(cipher);
    var el = document.getElementById('embed-demo');
    if (valid) {
      el.setAttribute('data-cipher', cipher);
      document.getElementById('embed-title').textContent = 'Interactive Demo — ' + cipher;
      document.title = cipher + ' Demo — The Cipher Museum';
    } else {
      el.innerHTML = '<p class="embed-error">No cipher specified. Use embed.html?cipher=caesar (or vigenere, playfair, railFence…)</p>';
    }
    // If the loader doesn't recognise the cipher, replace the spinner text.
    window.addEventListener('load', function () {
      setTimeout(function () {
        if (el.hasAttribute('data-cipher') && !el.querySelector('input, select, textarea, button')) {
          el.innerHTML = '<p class="embed-error">Unknown cipher "' + el.getAttribute('data-cipher').replace(/[^A-Za-z0-9]/g, '') + '". Check the demo name on its exhibit page.</p>';
        }
      }, 400);
    });
  })();
```
